{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5","id":"msg_011CdeFi6vHuUGyvmCjZfqud","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## Findings\n\n### 1. Where `tickMinStep` is read and wired in for continuous scales\n\n- **`/app/packages/vega-parser/src/parsers/axis.js:43`** — the axis parser reads the spec property and passes it into the `AxisTicks` transform's params:\n  ```js\n  minstep: scope.property(spec.tickMinStep),\n  ```\n  (Same pattern for legends at `/app/packages/vega-parser/src/parsers/legend.js:63`.)\n- **`/app/packages/vega-encode/src/AxisTicks.js:36`** — the transform pulls `_.minstep` out of its params and forwards it to `tickCount()`:\n  ```js\n  count = tickCount(scale, tally, _.minstep),\n  ```\n  (Legend equivalent: `/app/packages/vega-encode/src/LegendEntries.js:43`.)\n- Type declarations describing the intended semantics live in `/app/packages/vega-typings/types/spec/axis.d.ts:101-103` and the JSON schema in `/app/packages/vega-schema/src/axis.js:110`.\n\n### 2. The tick-generation function for continuous/linear scales\n\n- **`/app/packages/vega-scale/src/ticks.js`**:\n  - `tickCount(scale, count, minStep)` (lines 20–48) — computes the actual count/interval to hand to the scale's own `.ticks()` method. This is the function where `tickMinStep` is enforced.\n  - `tickValues(scale, count)` (lines 103–107) — calls `scale.ticks(count)` for scales that expose a `ticks` method (this is what continuous/linear/pow/sqrt/symlog d3 scales expose; log and time/utc scales have their own separate tick-generation branches — `tickLog()` at line 155, and time-interval handling inside `tickCount` itself at lines 40–45).\n  - The actual \"d3 continuous scale\" logic itself (linear/pow/sqrt) lives outside this repo's own source, in `node_modules/d3-scale/src/linear.js` + `node_modules/d3-array/src/ticks.js`. `scale.ticks(count)` there calls d3-array's `ticks(start, stop, count)`.\n\nSo the chain for a linear axis is:\n`axis.js` (parser) → `AxisTicks.js` (transform) → `vega-scale/src/ticks.js: tickCount()` → `scale.ticks(count)` (d3-scale, `linear.js` → d3-array `ticks.js`).\n\n### 3. The tick-step/minStep algorithm, and the ascending/descending asymmetry\n\nThe relevant code, `/app/packages/vega-scale/src/ticks.js:20-48`:\n\n```js\nexport function tickCount(scale, count, minStep) {\n  let step;\n\n  if (isNumber(count)) {\n    if (scale.bins) {\n      count = Math.max(count, scale.bins.length);\n    }\n    if (minStep != null) {\n      count = Math.min(\n        count,\n        Math.floor((span(scale.domain()) / minStep) || 1) + 1\n      );\n    }\n  }\n  ...\n  return count;\n}\n```\n\n`span()` (from `vega-util`, `/app/packages/vega-util/src/span.ts:7-9`) is a *signed* difference: `peek(array) - array[0]` — i.e. `domain[last] - domain[0]`, **not** an absolute value:\n\n```ts\nexport default function(array) {\n  return array && (peek(array) - array[0]) || 0;\n}\n```\n\n- For an **ascending** domain (e.g. `[0, 10]`), `span()` is positive (`10`), so `span/minStep` is a sensible positive number, and `Math.min(count, floor(...) + 1)` correctly clamps the tick count downward to respect the minimum step.\n- For a **descending/reversed** domain (e.g. `[10, 0]`, which happens whenever a continuous scale has `reverse: true` or the user explicitly supplies a domain with max before min), `span()` is **negative** (`-10`). Dividing by a positive `minStep` yields a negative number, so the computed replacement count becomes **negative or zero** (verified: for domain `[10,0]`, `minStep=1`, `count=10` → computed count is `-9`; with `minStep=2` → `-4`).\n- `Math.min(count, negativeNumber)` then returns that negative number as the new `count`, discarding the caller's real desired tick count entirely.\n- That negative count is passed straight into `scale.ticks(count)` (d3-scale linear → d3-array `ticks()`). D3-array's `ticks(start, stop, count)` (`/app/node_modules/d3-array/src/ticks.js`) begins with `if (!(count > 0)) return [];` — so a negative count causes **zero ticks to be generated at all**, not merely \"the minStep constraint being ignored.\"\n\nI verified this behavior directly:\n```\nascending domain [0,10], minStep=1, count=10  -> tickCount = 10   (correct)\ndescending domain [10,0], minStep=1, count=10 -> tickCount = -9   (broken)\ndescending domain [10,0], minStep=2, count=10 -> tickCount = -4   (broken)\n```\nAnd feeding these values into the real `d3.scaleLinear().domain([10,0])`:\n```\ns.ticks(10) -> [10,9,8,...,0]   (normal ticks)\ns.ticks(-9) -> []               (no ticks at all)\ns.ticks(-4) -> []               (no ticks at all)\n```\n\n### Hypothesis for why `tickMinStep` misbehaves on continuous scales with descending/reversed domains\n\nThe bug is in `tickCount()` in `/app/packages/vega-scale/src/ticks.js` (line 30): it uses the *signed* `span(scale.domain())` from `vega-util` rather than an absolute-value span (`Math.abs(...)`, or a `span` variant that already normalizes sign — note `axis.js`'s own signal expression for range explicitly does `abs(span(range(...)))` at `/app/packages/vega-parser/src/parsers/axis.js:107`, showing the codebase is aware elsewhere that `span` needs to be wrapped in `abs()` for reversed ranges, but this precaution was not applied in `tickCount`'s domain-span calculation). Because `scale.domain()` is not guaranteed to be ascending (any scale with `reverse: true`, or an inverted/descending domain specified by the user), `span()` can return a negative value here. That negative value flows into `Math.floor(negative/minStep)+1`, producing a negative \"clamped\" tick count, which is then handed to `scale.ticks(count)`. D3's continuous-scale ticks function treats any non-positive count as \"no ticks,\" so the entire tick set collapses to empty rather than the minStep constraint being merely \"off by a little.\" In short: **`tickMinStep` is enforced only correctly for ascending domains; for descending domains the missing `Math.abs()` around `span(scale.domain())` turns the minStep clamp into a tick-count killer.**"}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":411,"cache_read_input_tokens":32261,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":411,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01DCtFp4bgb9iVnt8ZfmTCmM","session_id":"b094bf93-ce2d-46e0-8000-81aa4d365a7b","uuid":"5fce4664-da3e-49a9-a4a2-17533f96d568","timestamp":"2026-08-02T16:41:44.685Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Find tickMinStep logic in codebase"}

timeInterval(count)\n      : scale.type == UTC ? utcInterval(count)\n      : error('Only time and utc scales accept interval strings.');\n    if (step) count = count.every(step);\n  }\n\n  return count;\n}\n\n/**\n * Filter a set of candidate tick values, ensuring that only tick values\n * that lie within the scale range are included.\n * @param {Scale} scale - The scale for which to generate tick values.\n * @param {Array<*>} ticks - The candidate tick values.\n * @param {*} count - The tick count or interval function.\n * @return {Array<*>} - The filtered tick values.\n */\nexport function validTicks(scale, ticks, count) {\n  let range = scale.range(),\n      lo = range[0],\n      hi = peek(range),\n      cmp = ascending;\n\n  if (lo > hi) {\n    range = hi;\n    hi = lo;\n    lo = range;\n    cmp = descending;\n  }\n\n  lo = Math.floor(lo);\n  hi = Math.ceil(hi);\n\n  // filter ticks to valid values within the range\n  // additionally sort ticks in range order (#2579)\n  ticks = ticks.map(v => [v, scale(v)])\n    .filter(_ => lo <= _[1] && _[1] <= hi)\n    .sort(cmp)\n    .map(_ => _[0]);\n\n  if (count > 0 && ticks.length > 1) {\n    const endpoints = [ticks[0], peek(ticks)];\n    while (ticks.length > count && ticks.length >= 3) {\n      ticks = ticks.filter((_, i) => !(i % 2));\n    }\n    if (ticks.length < 3) {\n      ticks = endpoints;\n    }\n  }\n\n  return ticks;\n}\n\n/**\n * Generate tick values for the given scale and approximate tick count or\n * interval value. If the\n * scale lacks a 'ticks' method, the full scale domain will be returned.\n * @param {Scale} scale - The scale for which to generate tick values.\n * @param {*} [count] - The approximate number of desired ticks.\n * @return {Array<*>} - The generated tick values.\n */\nexport function tickValues(scale, count) {\n  return scale.bins ? validTicks(scale, scale.bins, count)\n    : scale.ticks ? scale.ticks(count)\n    : scale.domain();\n}\n\n/**\n * Generate a label format function for a scale. If the scale lacks a\n * 'tickFormat' method, the returned formatter performs simple string coercion.\n * If the input scale is a logarithmic scale and the format specifier does not\n * indicate a desired decimal precision, a special variable precision formatter\n * that automatically trims trailing zeroes will be generated.\n * @param {Scale} scale - The scale for which to generate the label formatter.\n * @param {*} [count] - The approximate number of desired ticks.\n * @param {string} [specifier] - The format specifier. Must be a legal d3\n *   specifier string (see https://github.com/d3/d3-format#formatSpecifier) or\n *   time multi-format specifier object.\n * @return {function(*):string} - The generated label formatter.\n */\nexport function tickFormat(locale, scale, count, specifier, formatType, noSkip) {\n  const type = scale.type;\n  let format = defaultFormatter;\n\n  if (type === Time || formatType === Time) {\n    format = locale.timeFormat(specifier);\n  }\n  else if (type === UTC || formatType === UTC) {\n    format = locale.utcFormat(specifier);\n  }\n  else if (isLogarithmic(type)) {\n    const varfmt = locale.formatFloat(specifier);\n    if (noSkip || scale.bins) {\n      format = varfmt;\n    } else {\n      const test = tickLog(scale, count, false);\n      format = _ => test(_) ? varfmt(_) : '';\n    }\n  }\n  else if (scale.tickFormat) {\n    // if d3 scale has tickFormat, it must be continuous\n    const d = scale.domain();\n    format = locale.formatSpan(d[0], d[d.length - 1], count, specifier);\n  }\n  else if (specifier) {\n    format = locale.format(specifier);\n  }\n\n  return format;\n}\n\nexport function tickLog(scale, count, values) {\n  const ticks = tickValues(scale, count),\n        base = scale.base(),\n        logb = Math.log(base),\n        k = Math.max(1, base * count / ticks.length);\n\n  // apply d3-scale's log format filter criteria\n  const test = d => {\n    let i = d / Math.pow(base, Math.round(Math.log(d) / logb));\n    if (i * base < base - 0.5) i *= base;\n    return i <= k;\n  };\n\n  return values ? ticks.filter(test) : test;\n}\n","numLines":170,"startLine":1,"totalLines":170}}}

For an ascending domain (e.g. `[0, 12]`) this is fine, but for a descending/reversed domain (e.g. `[12, 0]`, which happens with `reverse: true` or a left-oriented axis in this bug report) `span()` returns a negative number. Dividing by `minStep` and flooring then yields a negative tick count, which gets passed straight to d3-scale's `scale.ticks(count)`. D3 treats any non-positive count as \"produce nothing\" for positive counts, but for negative-but-not-below-`Math.min`-clamped counts it can still return a set of ticks with spacing smaller than intended, since the `Math.min(count, ...)` clamp is no longer bounding `count` correctly — effectively the `tickMinStep` constraint stops constraining anything once the domain is descending.\n\n**Fix:** wrap the span computation in `Math.abs()` so the min-step distance is always treated as a positive interval regardless of domain direction:\n\n```js\n// packages/vega-scale/src/ticks.js\nif (minStep != null) {\n  count = Math.min(\n    count,\n    Math.floor((Math.abs(span(scale.domain())) / minStep) || 1) + 1\n  );\n}\n```\n\nThis matches a precaution already taken elsewhere in the codebase (`axis.js` wraps `span(range(...))` in `abs()` for reversed ranges), just missing here.\n\nI also added a regression test in `packages/vega-scale/test/ticks-test.js` verifying `tickCount` produces the same clamped count for both `[0, 12]` and `[12, 0]` domains with `minStep = 3`.
